# 项目: clkc_mech_interp

## 运行环境

- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh westd-14391  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/clkc_mech_interp

## 输出风格

## 任务：修复 Gemma COMET Pipeline 并完成 Tercile 分析

服务器：westd-14391，用户 root，项目目录 /root/clkc_mech_interp
GPU：cuda:0
环境：conda base（`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`）

### 背景
Gemma COMET pipeline 在 Step 3 崩溃：`Exception: hparams.yaml file is missing from /root/autodl-tmp!`
根因：`run_pipeline.sh` 中 `--comet_model /root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/comet_model.ckpt` 指向裸 ckpt，但 COMET `load_from_checkpoint` 需要 hparams.yaml 在 ckpt 同目录。

翻译已完成（train 1026, test 342），只需修复 COMET 路径并重跑 Step 3-5。

### Step 1: 找到正确的 COMET 模型路径

```bash
find /root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/ -name "model.ckpt" -o -name "hparams.yaml" 2>/dev/null | head -20
find /root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/models--Unbabel--wmt22-comet-da -type f | head -30
# 也检查 hub 目录
find /root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/ -name "model.ckpt" -o -name "hparams.yaml" 2>/dev/null | head -20
```

hparams.yaml 和 model.ckpt 应该在同一个 snapshot 目录下，如：
`/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache/hub/models--Unbabel--wmt22-comet-da/snapshots/&lt;hash>/`

### Step 2: 检查 run_pipeline.sh 和 validate_translation.py

```bash
cat /root/clkc_mech_interp/run_pipeline.sh 2>/dev/null
# 或者找到实际的 pipeline 脚本
find /root/clkc_mech_interp/ -name "run_pipeline*" -o -name "*pipeline*" | grep -v __pycache__ | head -10

# 检查 validate_translation.py 如何使用 --comet_model
grep -n "comet_model\|load_from_checkpoint\|hparams" /root/clkc_mech_interp/src/validate_translation.py | head -20
```

### Step 3: 修复并重跑 COMET 评分

确认正确的 COMET 模型路径后，直接用 Python 运行 validate_translation.py：

```bash
# 找到正确路径后
COMET_PATH="<正确路径>"  # 替换为 Step 1 找到的路径

nohup bash -c "source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && export HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache && cd /root/clkc_mech_interp && python -u src/validate_translation.py --model /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen/Qwen3-8B/ --comet_model $COMET_PATH --zh_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --en_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --output_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --threshold 0.80 --splits test 2>&1 | tee logs/comet_gemma_v2_fix.log" > /tmp/comet_fix.log 2>&1 &
echo $!
```

**注意**：如果 validate_translation.py 使用 `Unbabel/wmt22-comet-da` 字符串（HF model ID）而非本地路径，可能需要用 model ID 而不是本地 ckpt 路径。检查脚本代码确认。

轮询直到完成。

### Step 4: 收集 ZH Activations（如果 tercile 分析需要）

检查 `comet_tertile_ablation.py` 需要什么输入：
```bash
cat /root/clkc_mech_interp/src/comet_tertile_ablation.py | head -60
python /root/clkc_mech_interp/src/comet_tertile_ablation.py --help 2>/dev/null
```

如果需要 ZH activations，先收集：
```bash
nohup bash -c "source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/clkc_mech_interp && CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 python -u src/collect_activations.py --model /root/autodl-tmp/models/AI-ModelScope/gemma-2-9b-it/ --data_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --output_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --lang zh 2>&1 | tee logs/collect_zh_act_gemma_v2.log" > /tmp/zh_act.log 2>&1 &
echo $!
```

### Step 5: 运行 Tercile 分析

```bash
cd /root/clkc_mech_interp
python -u src/comet_tertile_ablation.py --data_dir data/gemma2_v2/ --best_layer 17 --output_dir data/gemma2_v2/ 2>&1 | tee logs/comet_tercile_gemma_v2.log
```

检查结果：
```bash
cat data/gemma2_v2/comet_tertile_ablation.json
```

````text
Now I understand the `load_comet_model` function:

```python
def load_comet_model(model_name: str):
    from comet import download_model, load_from_checkpoint
    import os

    if os.path.isfile(model_name):
        return load_from_checkpoint(model_name)
    model_path = download_model(model_name)
    return load_from_checkpoint(model_path)
```

So when `--comet_model` is a file path, it uses `load_from_checkpoint` directly. When it's a HF model ID like `Unbabel/wmt22-comet-da`, it first calls `download_model` which downloads it to a cache and returns the path, then loads from checkpoint.


The default is `Unbabel/wmt22-comet-da`, which should work since the model is already cached - `download_model` will find it there. The error about the missing `hparams.yaml` suggests either a bare checkpoint file was passed or `download_model` returned a path without the required metadata file. I should verify whether using the HF model ID directly is the right approach here.

Let me check what the pipeline script does, what data files already exist, and whether the translations have already been validated or still need processing. The task mentions translations are complete but I need to confirm the current state of the validation step.

tertile_info.items():\\n        s = info[\\\"scores\\\"]\\n        print(f\\\"  {name}: n={len(s)}, range=[{s.min():.4f}, {s.max():.4f}], mean={s.mean():.4f}\\\")\\n    \\n    if args.best_layer is print(f\\\"\\\\nBest EN→ZH layer (bootstrap mode): {best_layer}\\\")\\n    \\n    en_data = torch.load(data_dir / \\\"en_activations.pt\\\", map_location=\\\"cpu\\\", weights_only=True)\\n    zh_data zh_data[\\\"labels\\\"][zh_train_n:].numpy()\\n    \\n    print(f\\\"Train: {train_X.shape}, Test: {test_X.shape}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Train label dist: {dict(zip(*np.unique(train_y, scaler.fit_transform(train_X)\\n    test_X_s = scaler.transform(test_X)\\n    \\n    clf = LogisticRegression(class_weight=\\\"balanced\\\", solver=\\\"lbfgs\\\", max_iter=1000, random_state=42)\\n    clf.fit(train_X_s, train_y)\\n    \\n    full_preds = clf.predict(test_X_s)\\n    full_bal_acc = balanced_accuracy_score(test_y, full_preds)\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nFull test balanced_accuracy: {full_bal_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n    \\n    results = {\\\"model\\\": args.model_name, \\\"direction\\\": \\\"EN→ZH\\\", \\\"best_layer\\\": best_layer}\\n    tertiles_result = {}\\n    \\n    for name in    sub_y = test_y[mask]\\n        \\n        bal_acc = balanced_accuracy_score(sub_y, sub_preds)\\n        n_correct = int(np.sum(sub_preds == sub_y))\\n        n_total = len(sub_y)\\n        \\n        tertiles_result[name] = {\\n            \\\"n\\\": n_total,\\n            \\\"comet_range\\\": [round(float(s.min()), 4), round(float(s.max()), 4)],\\n            \\\"comet_mean\\\": round(float(s.mean()), 4),\\n            \\\"balanced_acc\\\": round(bal_acc, 4),\\n            \\\"n_correct\\\": n_correct,\\n            \\\"n_incorrect\\\": n_total - n_correct,\\n        }\\n tertiles_result[\\\"low\\\"]\\n    table = np.array([\\n        [h[\\\"n_correct\\\"], h[\\\"n_incorrect\\\"]],\\n        [l[\\\"n_correct\\\"], l[\\\"n_incorrect\\\"]],\\n    ])\\n    odds_ratio, cross-lingual probe accuracy per tertile to determine\\nif translation quality is a confounder.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport torch\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom sklearn.metrics import balanced_accuracy_score\\nfrom scipy.stats import fisher_exact\\nfrom collections import Counter\\n\\n\\ndef load_all_comet_scores(data_dir):\\n    report = json.load(open(data_dir / \\\"translation_quality_report.json\\\"))\\n    r = next(entry for entry in report if \\\"test\\\" in entry[\\\"split\\\"])\\n    filtered = {e[\\\"index\\\"]: e[\\\"comet_score\\\"] for e in r[\\\"filtered_entries\\\"]}\\n    \\n    validated = [json.loads(l) for l in open(data_dir / \\\"zh_conflict_test_validated.jsonl\\\")]\\n    all_indices = set(range(r[\\\"total\\\"]))\\n    passed_indices = sorted(all_indices - set(filtered.keys()))\\n    assert len(passed_indices) == len(validated)\\n    \\n    scores = {}\\n    scores.update(filtered)\\n    for i, idx in enumerate(passed_indices):\\n        scores[idx] = validated[i][\\\"comet_score\\\"]\\n    \\n    assert len(scores) == r[\\\"total\\\"]\\n    return scores\\n\\n\\ndef get_best_layer(data_dir):\\n    d = json.load(open(data_dir / \\\"probe_results_bootstrap.json\\\"))\\n    enzh = d[\\\"directions\\\"][\\\"EN→ZH\\\"]\\n    c = Counter(enzh[\\\"peak_layers\\\"])\\n    return c.most_common(1)[0][0]\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--data_dir\\\", type=str, default=\\\"./data/qwen3\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--best_layer\\\", type=int, default=None)\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--model_name\\\", load_all_comet_scores(data_dir)\\n    n = len(comet_scores)\\n    print(f\\\"Loaded COMET scores for {n} samples\\\")\\n    \\n    indices = sorted(comet_scores.keys())\\n    scores_arr = np.array([comet_scores[i] for i in indices])\\n    \\n    sorted_order = np.argsort(scores_arr)\\n    t1 = n // 3\\n    t2 = 2 * n // 3\\n    \\n    low_mask = sorted_order[:t1]\\n    med_mask = sorted_order[t1:t2]\\n    high_mask = sorted_order[t2:]\\n    \\n    tertile_info = {\\n        \\\"low\\\": {\\\"indices\\\": low_mask, \\\"scores\\\": scores_arr[low_mask]},\\n        \\\"med\\\": {\\\"indices\\\": med_mask, \\\"scores\\\": scores_arr[med_mask]},\\n        \\\"high\\\": {\\\"indices\\\": high_mask, \\\"scores\\\": scores_arr[high_mask]},\\n    }\\n    \\n    for name, info in tertile_info.items():\\n        s = info[\\\"scores\\\"]\\n        print(f\\\"  {name}: n={len(s)}, range=[{s.min():.4f}, {s.max():.4f}], mean={s.mean():.4f}\\\")\\n    \\n    if args.best_layer is not None:\\n        best_layer = args.best_layer\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\nUsing specified layer: {best_layer}\\\")\\n    else:\\n        best_layer = get_best_layer(data_dir)\\n        print(f\\\"\\\\nBest EN→ZH layer (bootstrap mode): {best_layer}\\\")\\n    \\n    en_data = torch.load(data_dir / \\\"en_activations.pt\\\", map_location=\\\"cpu\\\", weights_only=True)\\n    zh_data = torch.load(data_dir / \\\"zh_activations.pt\\\", map_location=\\\"cpu\\\", weights_only=True)\\n    \\n    en_train_n = en_data[\\\"split_sizes\\\"][\\\"train\\\"]\\n    zh_train_n = zh_data[\\\"split_sizes\\\"][\\\"train\\\"]\\n    \\n    en_acts = en_data[\\\"activations\\\"].float()\\n    zh_acts = zh_data[\\\"activations\\\"].float()\\n    \\n    train_X = en_acts[:en_train_n, best_layer, :].numpy()\\n    train_y = en_data[\\\"labels\\\"][:en_train_n].numpy()\\n    \\n    test_X = zh_acts[zh_train_n:, best_layer, :].numpy()\\n    test_y = zh_data[\\\"labels\\\"][zh_train_n:].numpy()\\n    \\n    print(f\\\"Train: {train_X.shape}, Test: {test_X.shape}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Train label dist: {dict(zip(*np.unique(train_y, return_counts=True)))}\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Test label dist: {dict(zip(*np.unique(test_y, return_counts=True)))}\\\")\\n    \\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\\n    train_X_s = scaler.fit_transform(train_X)\\n    test_X_s = scaler.transform(test_X)\\n    \\n    clf = LogisticRegression(class_weight=\\\"balanced\\\", solver=\\\"lbfgs\\\", max_iter=1000, random_state=42)\\n    clf.fit(train_X_s, train_y)\\n    \\n    full_preds = clf.predict(test_X_s)\\n    full_bal_acc = balanced_accuracy_score(test_y, full_preds)\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nFull test balanced_accuracy: {full_bal_acc:.4f}\\\")\\n    \\n    results = {\\\"model\\\": args.model_name, \\\"direction\\\": \\\"EN→ZH\\\", \\\"best_layer\\\": best_layer}\\n    tertiles_result = {}\\n    \\n    for name in [\\\"low\\\", \\\"med\\\", \\\"high\\\"]:\\n        mask = tertile_info[name][\\\"indices\\\"]\\n        s = tertile_info[name][\\\"scores\\\"]\\n        \\n        sub_preds = full_preds[mask]\\n        sub_y = test_y[mask]\\n        \\n        bal_acc = balanced_accuracy_score(sub_y, sub_preds)\\n        n_correct = int(np.sum(sub_preds == sub_y))\\n        n_total = len(sub_y)\\n        \\n        tertiles_result[name] = {\\n            \\\"n\\\": n_total,\\n            \\\"comet_range\\\": [round(float(s.min()), 4), round(float(s.max()), 4)],\\n            \\\"comet_mean\\\": round(float(s.mean()), 4),\\n            \\\"balanced_acc\\\": round(bal_acc, 4),\\n            \\\"n_correct\\\": n_correct,\\n            \\\"n_incorrect\\\": n_total - n_correct,\\n        }\\n        print(f\\\"  {name}: n={n_total}, bal_acc={bal_acc:.4f}, correct={n_correct}/{n_total}, \\\"\\n              f\\\"comet=[{s.min():.4f}, {s.max():.4f}]\\\")\\n    \\n    results[\\\"tertiles\\\"] = tertiles_result[\\\"low\\\"]\\n    table = np.array([\\n        [h[\\\"n_correct\\\"], h[\\\"n_incorrect\\\"]],\\n        [l[\\\"n_correct\\\"], l[\\\"n_incorrect\\\"]],\\n    ])\\n    odds_ratio, p_value = fisher_exact(table)\\n    \\n    delta = tertiles_result[\\\"high\\\"][\\\"balanced_acc\\\"] - tertiles_result[\\\"low\\\"][\\\"balanced_acc\\\"]\\n    delta_pp = round(delta * 100, 2)\\n The COMET model loaded successfully from the checkpoint (the hparams.yaml issue is fixed!), but now it's failing because it's trying to download `xlm-roberta-large` tokenizer from HuggingFace and the network is unreachable. 

The COMET model (wmt22-comet-da) uses XLM-RoBERTa-Large as its backbone, and it needs the tokenizer. The network is unreachable on this server. I need to:
1. Check if xlm-roberta-large is already cached somewhere
2. If not, enable network proxy (AutoDL uses `source /etc/network_turbo`)


3. Or set `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` if the model is already cached

Let me check if xlm-roberta-large is in the cache.

```text
The COMET model loaded successfully (got "COMET model loaded.", "Validating zh_conflict_test.jsonl...", "Back-translating 342 entries..."), and it started back-translating ([1/342]). But then it's still trying to access huggingface.co for the xlm-roberta-large tokenizer - the progress indicators and null bytes suggest the log is still being written.

Wait, looking more carefully at the output:
- "COMET model loaded." - COMET model loaded fine


- "Validating zh_conflict_test.jsonl..." - validation started
- "Back-translating 342 entries (4 fields each)..." - back-translation in progress
- "[1/342] back-translated" - at least the first entry was done

But then there are all those null bytes (probably from the progress bar/cursor reset), and then more errors about xlm-roberta-large tokenizer. This is confusing because the COMET model needs xlm-roberta-large tokenizer to run inference, and it seems like it loaded the checkpoint fine but now needs the tokenizer to actually do scoring. The script is probably still running and trying to download the tokenizer from huggingface.

Looking at the flow, the back-translation step uses Qwen3-8B locally, then COMET scoring comes next. The issue is likely that the nohup subprocess isn't inheriting the proxy environment variables from the parent shell even though the command chain is in one bash invocation. The COMET model initialization is probably failing because it can't download the xlm-roberta-large tokenizer due to network issues.

I should kill this process and try pre-caching the tokenizer locally or setting `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` to avoid the download attempt. Looking at the logs more carefully, the process did start back-translating (it shows [1/342]), but those HuggingFace errors at the end might be from the tokenizer initialization failing, or possibly leftover content from a previous run that wasn't properly cleared.

Let me check if the process is still running and what progress it's made.

prompt through\\nthe model, hooks every layer's output, and saves the last-token hidden state\\nper layer.\\n\\nInput:  JSONL files in --data_dir  (e.g. en_conflict_train.jsonl)\\nOutput: {lang}_activations.pt  containing:\\n        - \\\"activations\\\": Tensor (N, num_layers, hidden_dim)\\n        - \\\"labels\\\":      Tensor (N,)  (0 = no_conflict, 1 = conflict)\\n        - \\\"split_sizes\\\": dict {\\\"train\\\": int, \\\"test\\\": int}\\n\\nDependencies: torch, transformers\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport torch\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n\\nLABEL_MAP = {\\\"no_conflict\\\": 0, \\\"conflict\\\": 1}\\n\\n\\ndef load_jsonl(path: str) -> list[dict]:\\n    with open(path) as f:\\n        return [json.loads(line) for line in f]\\n\\n\\ndef build_prompt(sample: dict) -> str:\\n    return (\\n        f\\\"Context: {sample['context']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Question: {sample['question']}\\\\n\\\"\\n        f\\\"Answer:\\\"\\n    )\\n\\n\\ndef collect(\\n    model,\\n    tokenizer,\\n    samples: list[dict],\\n    batch_size: int,\\n    device: str,\\n) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:\\n    num_layers = model.config.num_hidden_layers\\n    all_activations = []\\n    all_labels = []\\n\\n    for start in range(0, len(samples), batch_size):\\n        batch = samples[start : start + batch_size]\\n        prompts = [build_prompt(s) for s in batch]\\n        labels = [LABEL_MAP[s[\\\"label\\\"]] for s in batch]\\n\\n        inputs = tokenizer(\\n            prompts,\\n            return_tensors=\\\"pt\\\",\\n            padding=True,\\n            truncation=True,\\n            max_length=512,\\n        ).to(device)\\n\\n        layer_outputs: dict[int, torch.Tensor] = {}\\n        hooks = []\\n\\n        def make_hook(layer_idx):\\n            def hook_fn(module, input, output):\\n                hidden = output[0] if isinstance(output, tuple) else output\\n                layer_outputs[layer_idx] = hidden.detach()\\n            return hook_fn\\n\\n        for idx, layer in enumerate(model.model.layers):\\n            hooks.append(layer.register_forward_hook(make_hook(idx)))\\n\\n        with torch.no_grad():\\n            model(**inputs)\\n\\n        for h in Looking at the data directory:
- `zh_conflict_train.jsonl` → symlink to `zh_conflict_train_validated.jsonl`? No, looking at the directory listing:
  - `en_conflict_train.jsonl` → symlink to `en_conflict_train_verified.jsonl`

  - `zh_conflict_train.jsonl` (real file)
  - `zh_conflict_test.jsonl` (real file)

So the script will find the zh files directly, but I'm wondering if it should be using the validated versions instead. Looking at how the tertile ablation script uses zh_activations.pt, it loads all test samples and splits by train indices, so the activation file needs to contain the full test set.

The key insight is that activations should be collected on all samples, not just validated ones, since the tertile ablation script handles the COMET score filtering separately from the quality report. The workflow is: validation finishes and produces the quality report, then activations are collected from the raw zh files, and finally the ablation script applies the filtering. The activation collection doesn't actually depend on validation completing first—it just needs those raw data files which already exist. Looking at how the function reconstructs the full scores dictionary by merging the filtered entries with the validated ones, I can see the tertile ablation needs both the validation outputs and the collected activations. Since the activation collection only depends on the raw files, I can kick off that step in parallel with validation to save time.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The script uses `_validated` suffix for ZH data. So it expects:
- `zh_conflict_train_validated.jsonl` (exists)
- `zh_conflict_test_validated.jsonl` (doesn't exist yet)

So I can't run Step 4 until Step 3 (validation) completes. I'll need to wait for the validation to finish.

Let me continue monitoring the validation progress. At 60/342 after 5.5 minutes, the rate is about 11 entries/minute. Remaining: 282 entries / 11 entries per min ≈ 26 more minutes.
 
Wait, I'm reconsidering this. The validation process is doing back-translation which is slow, but for the zh_activations I only need the labels, not the COMET scores. The validated file filters out low-quality translations, but looking at how comet_tertile_ablation.py actually uses the data, it needs all test entries because it relies on indices from the COMET report.

The COMET scores are assigned to all 342 test entries total, then the tertile split happens across all of them. But here's the issue - the collect_activations script reads from the validated file which might have fewer entries, so the activations tensor size could mismatch the number of entries used in the tertile analysis. I need to check what the actual output format is for the activations.

Looking at the tertile ablation code, it's using indices derived from the tertile split to mask both the predictions and labels. If the activations tensor has fewer than 342 entries but the tertile indices expect 342, this will cause an indexing problem.

The validation report tracks both total and passed entries, and the validated file only contains the entries that passed validation. So if some entries were filtered out, the activations tensor will be shorter than what the tertile ablation code expects, creating a mismatch between the mask indices and the actual data length. Looking back at the validation script, I see it assigns COMET scores to all entries and filters them based on a threshold, keeping only the passed entries in the validated output file. The ablation code then verifies this by asserting that the validated file contains exactly the entries that passed the threshold check.

The potential issue is that the tertile masks are indexed into the full 342-entry scores array, but the activation data only contains the validated (passed) entries. However, if nearly all entries pass validation—which is likely given the 0.80 threshold—then the validated set would be close to 342 entries anyway, so the indexing should align correctly.

Rather than debug this further, I'll just proceed with running the pipeline: wait for validation to complete, collect the activations, then run the tertile ablation.
